失败题最忌讳两件事：编一个没发生过的失败，或者讲一个"其实不是我错"的假失败。你双十一那次是真的判断错了，而且错在你最擅长的数据领域，这最有说服力。 ■ 讲法（S-T-A-R + 反思） S：24 年双十一，我负责品牌天猫渠道的站内广告投放。当时我们放量最多、ROI 最高的点位是超级直播，只作用于品牌自播间。开播前三个小时，ROI 已经到 7 以上，接近平时的两倍。 T：我的任务是让投放效率最大化，所以按原计划，我应该继续加投。 A：我当时就加投了。这是我做错的地方，我看到 ROI=7 就相信了它，没有先验证这个数字背后的生意是不是真的涨了。 我是三个小时之后才发现问题的。当时我为了盯最大爆发期，让代运营按小时报数，我在第三个小时的数字里看到：达人直播间完成率接近 110%，而我自己的品牌自播间不到 50%。这跟我在投放看板上看到的 ROI=7 完全对不上。 下钻之后发现是归因问题：它是末次点击归因，消费者在自播间被广告曝光，然后去比价、在更划算的达人直播间下单，钱算在了我的点位上，但我的直播间一单没多。 我做的第一个动作是先停投，第二个动作是把省下来的预算挪 30% 到搜索类的点位（入口不同，避免人群重叠和跳转比价），第三个动作才是下钻分析。 R：自播间的生意救回来了。但这个结果的代价是我浪费了三个小时的爆发期预算。 R+反思：这件事之后我改了一个习惯，一直用到现在：任何一个指标突然变好，我的第一反应不是加投，是先去找一个能交叉验证它的独立数字。ROI 涨了，我就去看分渠道的生意完成率；点击率涨了，我就去看转化率有没有跟上。如果只有一个数字在涨而其他数字没动，那这个"涨"多半是口径问题，不是生意问题。 我把这个习惯带到了后面所有的投放决策里。说实话，我现在对数据有那种"归因洁癖"，是被这一次烫出来的。 ■ 如果面试官追问"现在再遇到你会怎么做" 我会先花十分钟把分渠道的生意数字拉出来，再决定加不加投。因为大促爆发期的时间很贵，但它没有"加投错了"贵，加投错了，钱花出去了还买不到增量，而且会让我误判整个大促的结构。

English · spoken script

Double Eleven last year. I was running on-site advertising for the brand on Tmall. Our highest-volume, highest-ROI placement was a livestream product that only served the brand's own livestream room. Three hours into the broadcast, ROAS was already above seven, nearly double our normal. My job was to maximize efficiency, so according to the plan I'd made, I should have kept adding budget. And I did. That's the mistake. I saw a ROAS of seven and I believed it, without first checking whether the business behind that number had actually grown. I only caught it three hours later. I'd asked the agency to report hourly so I could watch the peak window, and in the third hour I saw this: the affiliate livestream rooms were at about 110% of target, and my own brand livestream room was under 50%. That doesn't reconcile with a ROAS of seven. So I dug in, and it was an attribution problem. The product used last-click. A user would get exposed to the ad inside my brand livestream, then go price-compare and buy in a cheaper affiliate room. The revenue got credited to my placement; my room didn't sell a single extra unit. The first thing I did was pause the spend. The second was move 30% of the freed budget into a search-style placement, different entry point, so no audience overlap and no hopping to compare prices. Only the third thing was the deep-dive analysis. We recovered the brand room's business. But the cost of that outcome was three hours of peak-window budget, wasted. What I changed afterward, and I still do this today: if any metric suddenly improves, my first response is not to add budget. It's to find one independent number that can cross-check it. ROAS goes up, I go look at channel-level sales attainment. Click-through goes up, I check whether conversion followed. If one number is moving and everything else is flat, that "improvement" is almost always a measurement artifact, not business. And honestly, the reason I have that attribution allergy now is that this one burned me.

中文

【故事背景】刚入职腾讯时，我接手家清纸品的头部客户，但这个团队过去几乎只做美妆：客户不熟，对口的销售也不熟。更麻烦的是，我们中台和销售的 KPI 是冲突的，我背品牌 GMV，他们背客户的广告消耗。双方都觉得对方在抢自己的资源。 ■ 讲法 S：我刚接手一个全新的行业，客户不熟，对口的销售也不熟，而且我们两边 KPI 不一样，我看品牌生意增长，他们看客户在广告上的投入。这种结构下，双方天然会把信息当筹码，不会主动共享。 T：我有 24 个品牌要负责，如果靠自己一个个去 cold call 建联，三个月也摸不完，而且我拿不到客户真实的情况。 A：我没有先去找客户，我先问了自己三个问题：我能做什么、销售需要什么、客户需要什么。 然后我发现一件事：我们部门是唯一能产出行业级数据的团队，而广告 BU 和小店 BU 之间的数据流通很闭塞。大客户销售每天都在陪跑单个客户，反而没时间看整个大盘的趋势和热点：这既是他们缺的，也是我手上唯一的、别人给不了的东西。 所以我第一步不是去要客户名单，是先给销售一份行业大盘的趋势和机会点。我给的是价值，不是请求。 拿到信任之后，我才开始问客户的情况。但我没有让销售一个个讲，而是请他们帮我一起做客户分层。分层的方式我没有按 GMV 降序，那样销售不会参与，因为那只体现我的 KPI。我用了三个维度：生意规模、组织架构成熟度、商业化投入意愿。第三个维度是他们的 KPI，这样他们就有动力跟我一起做。 切出来四类客户之后，我对每一类用不同的运营方式：规模大投入意愿高的做深度定制；规模大但意愿低的先找痛点；规模小但意愿高的给标准化方案；规模小意愿低的用批量手段覆盖。 R：三个月之内，24 个头部品牌里我跟 10 个建立了深度合作，还通过 T2T 会议推动了品牌内部架构的调整。更重要的是，销售从"不给我信息"变成了"主动拉我进客户会议"。 ■ 迁移句（必须补） 这件事放到 Google 是一样的：大客户身上最难的不是方案，是"客户内部谁会替你说话"。我的习惯是先搞清楚每个人要什么，然后从"我能给什么"开始，而不是从"我需要什么"开始。

English · spoken script

When I joined, I took over the head accounts in the home-care and paper category, but the team had almost only ever worked on beauty. I didn't know the clients, and I didn't know the salespeople who owned them. And on top of that, our KPIs were in conflict: I carried brand GMV growth, they carried the client's advertising spend. In that structure, both sides naturally treat information as leverage and don't share it. I had twenty-four brands to cover. If I cold-called my way in one at a time, three months wouldn't be enough, and I still wouldn't get the real picture of what was going on with each client. So I didn't start with the clients. I started with three questions to myself: what can I do, what does sales need, what does the client need. And then I found the one thing I had. Our team was the only one that could produce category-level data, the data flow between the ads organization and the store organization was almost nonexistent. Large-client salespeople spend every day running alongside individual accounts, so they never have time to look at the whole category's trends and hotspots. That was both their gap and the only asset I had that nobody else could give them. So my first move wasn't asking for a client list. It was handing sales a view of the category's trends and opportunities. I gave value before I asked for anything. Once I had trust, I asked about the clients. But I didn't ask them to brief me one by one, I asked them to help me segment the book. And I deliberately did not sort by GMV, because that only reflects my KPI and sales wouldn't engage. I used three dimensions: business scale, how mature the client's organization was, and willingness to invest commercially. That third one is their KPI, which is what gave them a reason to build this with me. That produced four tiers, and I serviced each differently: large and willing gets deep customization; large but unwilling gets pain-point discovery first; small but willing gets a standardized playbook; small and unwilling gets scaled coverage. Within three months I'd built deep working relationships with ten of the twenty-four head brands, and I'd driven internal org changes at some of them through top-to-top meetings. But the bigger outcome was that sales went from withholding information to pulling me into client meetings themselves. And the transfer: on large clients, the hard part isn't the proposal, it's who inside the client will speak for it. My habit is to work out what each person wants, and to start from what I can give, not from what I need.

中文

【为什么这题要单独列】LCS 大中华区的岗位，日常工作就是"跟中国客户用中文谈生意、跟全球团队用英文对齐"，面试官大概率会考一次跨文化/跨语言的真实场景。而这题我没法替你编，你没跟我说过具体哪一次，我不能替你造一个。 ■ 答题骨架（照这个顺序讲，STAR 不变） 1. 场景里必须有一个真实的"落差"：不是"语言不通"，而是更具体的东西，比如对方默认的决策节奏跟你不一样、对同一句话的理解不一样、或者书面和口头表达的权重不一样。 2. 你做了什么来消除这个落差（这是重点，不要只说"我英文好"）。 3. 结果：对方的反应变了，或者事情的效率变了。 ■ 三个你可以挑的素材方向（选你真的经历过的那个） 方向 A：在欧莱雅（外企）用英文跟总部/跨部门对齐，同时要把结论翻成中国团队能落地的动作。落差在于"总部的战略语言"和"中国电商的执行语言"是两套。 方向 B：在腾讯跟跨境/出海业务相关的场景（如果有的话），中英切换中出现的理解偏差。 方向 C：你准备这轮面试的过程本身，比如同一件事用中文讲和用英文讲，重心其实不一样（中文重关系与语境，英文重结论与结构），你意识到这一点之后是怎么调整的。 ■ 如果没有真实的大场景，就诚实地说小的 不要为了讲故事而放大一件小事。你可以直接讲一个具体的、小的沟通误会，以及你怎么处理的，真实的细节比宏大的故事可信。 ■ 收尾句（无论用哪个素材，都补这句） 我的体会是：双语工作的难点从来不是"翻得准不准"，是"你知不知道对方真正要的是什么"。同一句话，中国客户听到的是关系和面子，全球团队听到的是结论和依据。我做的事就是在两边之间做翻译，不只是语言的翻译，是意图的翻译。 【英文版（骨架，把 [ ] 里的部分换成你的真实经历）】 Let me give you a real one. [场景：什么时候、跟谁、用什么语言、要做成什么]. The gap wasn't language, it was [具体的落差：节奏 / 表达权重 / 默认假设]. What I did was [你具体做的动作]. And the result was [对方的反应或效率的变化]. What I've learned doing this is that bilingual work isn't really about translating accurately. It's about knowing what the other person actually needs from you. The same sentence, a Chinese client hears relationship and face, a global team hears conclusion and evidence. What I do is translate intent, not just words.

中文

大客户 AM 经常要说 no：对客户说"这个预算我不建议这么花"，对内部说"这个产品现在不适合推"。怎么说 no，决定对方觉得你靠谱，还是觉得你在挡路。 ■ 讲法 S：还是双十一。当时我们 ROI 最高的点位表现特别好，按原计划要继续加投，而且代运营和投手的意见都是加投，因为看板上的数字支持他们。 T：我要让他们在爆发期停下来，而且是往回收，不是少加一点。在一个所有人都在追量的节点上叫停，阻力是很大的。 A：我没有直接说"不能投"，我做了三件事。 第一，我先拿出一个他们没法反驳的独立数字：分渠道的生意完成率，达人直播间接近 110%，而我们自己的品牌自播间不到 50%。这个数字不是我的判断，是事实，所以它比我讲道理有用。 第二，我给的不是一个"停"，是一个"换成什么"。我当场给出了替代方案：把省下来的预算挪 30% 到搜索类的点位。理由我也讲清楚了，入口和直播间完全不同，可以最大程度避免人群重叠和跳转比价。也就是说，我不是在阻止他们花钱，我是在把钱挪到一个更可能有效的地方。 第三，我把这个决定做成可验证的：我说清楚挪过去之后看什么指标、多久看一次，如果两周内没起色我们再调回来。这让他们不用承担"听我的结果错了"的风险。 R：自播间的生意救回来了，而且这套"大促/平销错峰、不同入口错配"的思路后来在每一次平台大促都沿用。 ■ 迁移句 我现在对"说 no"有一套固定的做法：永远不说"不行"，永远说"如果换成这样，你能得到什么"。因为对方要的不是一个拒绝，是一个更好的选项。而且我拒绝的时候一定会同时给出验证方式，这样即使我判断错了，也能在两周内被发现，不会拖到季度复盘。

English · spoken script

Same Double Eleven. Our highest-ROI placement was performing beautifully and the plan said keep adding budget. The agency and the media operators all wanted to add more, because the dashboard supported them. What I needed was for them to stop during the peak window, and not just add less, actually pull back. Calling a halt at the exact moment everyone is chasing volume is very unpopular. So I didn't say "we can't spend." I did three things. First, I brought an independent number they couldn't argue with: channel-level sales attainment, affiliate livestream rooms at nearly 110% of target, our own brand livestream room under 50%. That's not my opinion, that's a fact, and it's more useful than me explaining a theory. Second, I didn't offer a stop, I offered a swap. Right there I gave the alternative: move 30% of the freed budget into a search-style placement. And I explained why, completely different entry point, so it avoids audience overlap and people hopping off to compare prices. In other words, I wasn't blocking their spend, I was moving it somewhere more likely to work. Third, I made the decision testable. I told them exactly what metric we'd watch, how often, and that if nothing moved within two weeks we'd move it back. That removed the risk of "we listened to you and it was wrong." We recovered the brand room's business, and the logic behind it, staggering peak versus non-peak, mismatching entry points, became the standard approach for every major promotion afterward. And the transfer: I have a fixed way of saying no now. I never say "that won't work." I say "if we do it this way instead, here's what you get." Because what the other person needs isn't a refusal, it's a better option. And whenever I say no, I always attach a way to verify it, so if I'm wrong, it surfaces within two weeks instead of at the quarterly review.

我认为是入职腾讯之后，作为owner完成并落地了个护家清行业在微信小店的经营方法论。我想先具体阐述一下这个项目，再说为什么我认为他最为成功 我们团队作为策略中台，既需要产出单客户的经营方案，也需要沉淀全行业通用的打法策略，我作为个护家清类目的负责人，服务的客户统一被称作k800，一共43个，年GMV规模在50亿左右。他们是微信电商下平台圈选的核心商家，品牌本身无论在站内还是站外都已经积累了足够的销量和声量。我到来之后面临的第一个问题就是：如何建联客户。因为我们团队是去年才成立，在我入职之前并没有人负责个护这个行业，因此客户资源可以说是从0开始积累，我需要在短时间内快速和所有的客户建联，按照这个量级和客户的体量来说很不现实，所以我做的第一件事就是根据品牌的GMV规模以及广告消耗对他们做一个四象限分层，之所以要参考广告消耗，是因为我需要借销售的力量实现快速覆盖，如果一个个cold call客户，效率过低且成效不大，所以我选择用GMV x 广告销售的逻辑，站在销售的角度去对客户进行层级划分。在筛选出头部客户之后，我通过T2T规划共创，workshop，深度陪跑等形式，与10个头部客户快速拉近了客情，而对于腰尾部客户的渗透，我则利用头部客户经营方面的信息差加上定期的行业分析诊断以及大盘趋势批量的做快速覆盖，再借助线下展会去加深客情 在做到客户的全量覆盖之后，下一个问题就是如何产出适用于个护家清行业的通用方法论，因为没有之前的经验参考，我决定从四象限客户分层结合三大子赛道个人护理，家清，纸品的经营模式以及成交结构，总结不同阶段下客户的共性&特色问题，再基于大盘表现梳理行业机会点。对于个护行业的品牌来说，KOC的铺量覆盖和质量管控，以及货架场景的潜在增量是他们未来最重要的机会点。在做完内部的输出之后，我就去找对应阶段的不同品牌去落地方法论，并不断收集他们的反馈做迭代。同时和销售一起讨论在广告创收维度上的商机。目前基于方法论，我帮助滴露，沫檬，维达在达播分销成交的GMV绝对值上做到了环比提升40%，清风和德佑在货架场景的成交占比提高了5pts，并且回购率提高了3pts。目前这个方法论还在不断的覆盖更多k800品牌，帮助他们在普适性的方法论指导下更快的获取视频号增量，同时基于单一客户，我也会在方法论的基础上做深度的延展和复盘。 为什么我认为最成功，我认为可以从以下三点去剖析： · 从个人成长来看：我完成了品牌到平台的视角切换，通过合理的客户分层以及内部协作快速实现了客户覆盖，并获得了cross function的认可，也为我之后的工作奠定了自我的方法论 · 从团队层面来看：它帮助我们team完成了个护家清行业从0到1的突破，从之前无人以经营的角色负责，到获得客户以及销售的信任，为我们团队积累了一定的reputation · 从最终结果来说：我成功将个护的43个客户经营模式总结成了可复用的方法论，并推动落实，不仅实现了单客的生意增长，还推动了整个行业在新机会点上的探索。如今个护行业短视频成交占比已成为小店TOP1，越来越多的标杆案例在被传播和复用

The one I'd point to is this: after I joined Tencent, I owned and shipped the operating playbook for the personal care and home care category on WeChat Shop. Let me lay out what the project actually was first, then tell you why I'd call it the most successful thing I've done. Our team sits as a strategy hub. Two jobs: produce operating plans for individual clients, and distill plays that work across the whole industry. I own personal care and home care. The clients I cover all sit under one internal label, K800: forty-three of them, around five billion RMB in annual GMV. They're the core merchants the platform hand-picks, and by the time I got there these brands already had real scale and brand presence, on WeChat and off it. The first problem I hit was simple and brutal: how do I even get in front of these people? Our team was only set up the year before, and nobody had owned personal care before me, so my client relationships were starting from literally zero. Forty-three clients at that size, in a short window — doing it one cold call at a time wasn't going to happen. So the first thing I built was a four-quadrant segmentation, plotting GMV against ad spend. I pulled ad spend in on purpose: I needed the sales org to help me cover ground fast. If I cold-called everyone myself it would've been slow and mostly wasted. Framing it as GMV times ad sales meant I was building the tiers from the sales team's point of view, and that's what got them moving with me. Once the head accounts were identified, I went deep with ten of them: joint planning sessions, workshops, hands-on support all the way through execution. That closed the relationship quickly. For the mid-tail and long-tail I used a different lever — the insight gap I'd picked up from working with the head accounts, plus regular industry diagnostics and platform-wide trend readouts, to cover them in bulk. Then offline events to deepen things from there. So coverage solved. Next problem: how do you actually produce a playbook that holds for personal care and home care as a category? There was nothing to reference, no earlier version. So I started from the four-quadrant segmentation, crossed it with how the three sub-verticals actually run — personal care, home care, tissue — and with how their transactions break down. From that I pulled the problems that were common at each stage and the ones that were specific to a given category, then layered the platform-wide data on top to find where the opportunity really was. For personal care brands, the two biggest levers going forward are KOC volume coverage and quality control, and the untapped growth in shelf scenarios. Once we had the internal version, I took it to brands at different stages and ran it with them, collected their feedback, and iterated. In parallel I sat with sales to work out where the advertising revenue opportunity sat in all of this. Off the back of the playbook, I helped Dettol, Momeng and Vinda grow their creator-distribution GMV by forty percent quarter on quarter; for Breeze and Deeyeo, the shelf-scenario share of transactions went up five points and repeat purchase rate up three points. The playbook is still rolling out to more K800 brands — the idea is that they get to WeChat Channels growth faster under a method that's already proven. And on top of that, for individual clients I go deeper and run a proper review on top of the playbook. So why do I call it the most successful? Three angles. For me personally: I made the switch from a brand-side view to a platform-side view. I got to full coverage through sensible segmentation and internal collaboration, earned cross-functional recognition, and came out of it with a working method of my own that I've used ever since. For the team: it got us from zero to one in personal care and home care. Before this, nobody owned the category in an operating role; now we have the trust of both the clients and the sales org. That's reputation the team didn't have before. And on the result: I turned how forty-three personal care clients operate into a playbook other people can actually reuse, and I drove the adoption. Individual clients grew, and the category as a whole moved into opportunity areas nobody had mapped. Personal care's short-video share of transactions is now number one across WeChat Shop, and more and more benchmark cases are getting circulated and reused.

中文

25年8月的天猫超品，我负责天猫自播，当时单场的目标是600w，同比去年有15%的涨幅，我最终landing到了700w，虽然结果是好的， 但是在直播过程中，出现了前期准备不充足，临场调整不充分的问题，也为我之后的工作做了警示 背景是品牌在8月有一场全年最大的明星campaign，是生意与品宣同步的大促活动。在基于去年同期的表现之后，我决定把今年campaign的自播目标定在涨幅15%，这是基于过往明星节点的涨幅规律以及今年品牌整体的增长趋势算出来的增长幅度，是一个stretch但同时又有机会达成的目标。在有了目标之后，我便开始着手准备起当天直播大场的rundown，主播话术妆造，直播置景等等。 但是等到了bigday当天，幸福的意外还是发生了，由于我们代言人肖战的粉丝过于热烈的反响，原定4个小时的直播带货，在开播后15分钟，我的主推品就全部售罄，直播间一度面临长达20分钟无货可播，只能临时让运营商新建链接重新做图上架有货的产品。这并不是我备货不充足，而是在前期规划链接的时候，过度复杂的加赠导致单个链接库存太少，而重新准备链接涉及到提报活动以及素材图的设计，这些加在一起至少20分钟以上。当时直播间实时在线人数一度从10w下跌到3w。在这20分钟里我是手足无措没有任何backup plan的，只能去按照缺货补货的策略机械执行。在最后重新上架链接之后，直播间的销量才重新抬头。最终的landing在前一小时完成了生意的80%，后三小时通过权重的加持完成了剩下的20% 我之所以想要分享这个案例，是因为这次bigday的失利为我后面无论在电商运营还是平台小二的角色上，都带来了非常重要的影响，在复盘之后，我有3个learning 第一：在运营上化繁为简，这次库存售罄最大的根因就在于我什么都想要，因此把送赠链接做的纷乱复杂，粉丝弄不清楚如何下单，连我们运营人员也弄不清楚，有的时候过度的复杂往往会给消费者带来更差的体验，所以有了这次的经验之后，我在天猫的运营商，机制都以简单明了出街，并不会去做过度复杂的组合 第二：永远在PLAN A的基础上做PLAN B&C。在吸取了这次经验之后，无论是在品牌还是平台，我给出的方案都会想好预案，例如在腾讯推进客户在短视频场景发力时，我会以一个主推的机构组合其他多个备选机构一起推荐给客户，避免鸡蛋放在一个篮子里从而出现意外导致无法补救 第三：从错误中发现机会，虽然由于失误导致出现了20分钟空窗，但是在后续复盘中，我发现无论是弹幕还是站外社交媒体，都出现了粉丝关于品牌的正向评论，大家探讨的不是为什么品牌没有做好准备，而是在为自家偶像惊人的带货力而称赞，从数据角度也可以看出，虽然实时场观下跌了2/3，但是在线停留时常提高了50%，说明真正想买而没有买到的人，依然蹲守在直播间等待补货，而缺货的这个事实，反而成为了促进粉丝购买的催化剂。所以在后续的运营中，无论是我自己，还是给到品牌的建议，我都会让他们在大场的时候准备好逼单话术，确保转化率下跌的时候可以快速拉升一波 所以我认为虽然这对我来说，是一场准备不充分的失败，但是他对我未来的工作具有非常好的警示和借鉴意义

English · spoken script

The failure I'd point to is Tmall Super Brand Day last August. I owned the brand's own livestream. The target for that one session was six million RMB, up fifteen percent on the year before, and I landed at seven million. So the number came out fine. But the session itself wasn't — I under-prepared, and I adjusted badly in the moment. That's what makes it the one worth talking about. Context: the brand runs its biggest celebrity campaign of the year in August, a moment that's supposed to deliver sales and brand heat at the same time. Working off how the comparable session performed last year, I set the self-broadcast target at plus fifteen percent. That came from the historical lift on celebrity moments plus where the brand was tracking this year. A stretch goal, but a reachable one. Once the target was locked I got on with the rundown for the big session, host scripts, styling, set design, all of it. Then the day came, and a happy accident turned into a problem. Our spokesperson is Xiao Zhan, and his fans turned out far harder than we'd planned for. We had a four-hour selling window. Fifteen minutes in, every hero SKU was sold out. The room went twenty minutes with nothing to sell, and the only move left was to have the agency build new product links and re-shoot the listing images on the spot. It wasn't a stock problem, it was a link problem: I'd built the gift-with-purchase mechanics so elaborately that each individual link only carried a small slice of inventory, and rebuilding a link means resubmitting it into the campaign and redoing the creative — twenty minutes at minimum, all of it stacked. Concurrent viewers fell from a hundred thousand to thirty thousand. And for those twenty minutes I had nothing. No backup plan. I just executed the restock procedure mechanically. Links came back up and sales recovered. The final shape of the session: eighty percent of the business in the first hour, the remaining twenty percent across the next three hours on platform traffic weighting. I'm telling this one because it changed how I work on both sides of the desk — brand e-commerce ops, and now the platform side. Three things came out of the post-mortem. First: keep the mechanics simple. The root cause of that sellout was that I wanted everything at once, so the gifting structure got tangled. Fans couldn't work out how to place an order. Neither could our own operators. Complexity that clever usually lands as a worse experience for the person buying. Since then, on Tmall, our mechanics go out simple and explicit. I don't build over-engineered bundles anymore. Second: there's always a plan B and a plan C on top of plan A. After this, every plan I put out has a fallback built into it, brand side or platform side. Concretely: when I push clients to lean into short-form video on WeChat Channels now, I put one primary agency in front of them alongside several alternates, so one thing going wrong can't leave them with nothing to fall back on. Third: find the opportunity inside the mistake. We lost twenty minutes, but in the review the comments — in the livestream chat and off-platform — were positive. People weren't asking why the brand wasn't ready. They were talking about how hard their idol could move product. And the data said the same thing: concurrent viewership dropped by two thirds, but average watch time went up fifty percent. The people who genuinely wanted to buy and missed out stayed parked in the room waiting for restock. Being sold out became a catalyst. So now, in my own work and in what I recommend to brands, big sessions always have closing scripts ready, so the moment conversion dips you can pull it back up. So yes, it was a failure of preparation. And it's the most useful warning I carry into the work.

反向提问清单，和说数字之前要守的口径（2 条）

中文

■ 问 HR（第一轮） 1. 这个岗位的 base 是上海还是北京？（必须问，第一轮就问清楚） 2. 这个组除了 SHEIN，其他 app retail 客户大概是什么体量、多少家？ 3. 面试流程还有几轮、每一轮考什么？ ■ 问 hiring manager（最关键，挑 2-3 个） 1. 您理想中这个人接手 SHEIN 六个月后，做成什么样子，您会觉得"招对了"？ （这题必问，它直接告诉你他的评价标准，你后面所有回答都可以往这个标准上靠） 2. 这个客户现在的挑战里，哪一个是您觉得最难、前任一直没解决的？ （这题能问出真实痛点，而且显示你是来解决问题的，不是来找工作的） 3. 这个组是纯 AM，还是 AM + 分析师 + 技术支持的 pod 模式？我跟哪些内部角色配合？ 4. 您怎么判断一个新 AM 在这个客户身上"站稳了"？有没有一个具体的时间点和信号？ 5. 上一个在这个位置上做得好的人，做对了什么？ （这题的答案往往就是这份工作真正的 KPI） ■ 问未来的同事 / peer 1. 你日常跟 SHEIN 这类客户对接，花时间最多的是哪一类事？ 2. 在这个团队，什么事情是你觉得最耗人、但没人解决的？ ■ 不要问的 · 不要问"这个岗位为什么空着"（可能涉及前任离职等敏感信息） · 不要问薪资福利（留给 HR 阶段） · 不要问"你们觉得我表现怎么样"（当场要评价是大忌） · 不要在 HR 面前问太深的业务题，在 hiring manager 面前问太浅的流程题 ■ 一个加分问法（最后一轮用） "如果我入职，前三个月您最希望我不要做的一件事是什么？" ，这题会让对方停下来认真想，而且答案通常非常有用。

中文

■ 铁律：区分三种口径，说的时候带上前缀 1. 公司披露（港交所公告/招股书/业绩公告）→ 说"它在财报里披露的是……" 2. 券商梳理/估算 → 说"券商大致估的是……"（GMV、地区结构、模式占比都属于这一类） 3. 第三方数据/媒体报道 → 说"我看到的市场数据是……"（份额变化、日活变化） 混着说会出事。比如"GMV 315 亿"是券商估算，不是公司披露的净收入（上半年净收入是 201 亿），这两个数字不是一回事，说混了会被懂行的人当场抓到。 ■ 五个最容易说错的坑 1. 净收入 vs GMV：201 亿美元是净收入，315 亿美元是 GMV 估算，差着一个自营/平台的口径。 2. +1% vs +6.4%：收入涨 1%、订单涨 6.4%，这两个数字要成对出现，只说一个会得出相反结论。 3. 欧洲 -13.9% 的归因：公司自己的说法是"提价 + 主动降低线上广告投放"，这是官方归因，不是你的推断，说的时候要标清楚。 4. 估值对比：982 亿是 2022 年私募融资时的估值，265 亿是这次香港 IPO 的估值口径。两个都不是"市值"的同一定义，说"估值从 X 降到 Y"可以，别说是"市值蒸发"。 5. 免税政策时间：美国是 2025 年 5 月，欧盟是 2026 年 7 月。别记反。 ■ 三条不能碰的红线 1. 不要说任何"我了解到的内部信息"。你没有，也不要装作有。 2. 不要对 SHEIN 的经营决策做道德评价（环保、劳工争议等）。面试官问到，你就说"这些是它面临的外部舆论环境，也是它品牌升级必须处理的问题"，然后转回生意。 3. 不要承诺具体的 ROAS/收入数字。竞价市场的价格不是你能定的。 ■ 关于你自己数字的一致性 你故事里的数字（1300w、180w、+9%、85%、100 倍、75%、10 个品牌/24 个）在这轮必须跟上一轮完全一致。Google 内部有面试记录，前后不一致是最容易被抓的硬伤。如果上一轮你说过某个数字，这一轮改了，面试官会当场问。 ■ 地点 这一轮无论对方问不问，你都要在某个环节主动把 base 说清楚，并确认岗位地点。上一轮就是栽在这里，不要让它再发生第二次。
